# Lens I, area 1: the guard, the launcher and the sandbox — a review of their tests against the specifications in force

Independent assessment of the Gov OS, October 2026, lens I (the test review ordered by DEC-889 item 16), area 1 of 4.

## In brief

- **What I reviewed.** 540 test functions with 3,844 cases, out of the area's 2,042 functions and 9,896 cases. That is every failing or questionable test, every suite whose feature a later decision changed, a seeded 6 % sample of the rest, and a static scan of all 2,042 for shapes that cannot fail. The area is 63 % of the estate's cases and 32 % of its parallel case time.
- **The verdicts:** 465 valid and needed, 12 duplicate, 11 flaky, 34 outdated, 15 obsolete, 3 weak (of 540 functions). Most of the area is sound: W2-58's test designer had already rewritten about 1,230 W1-02 cases to Policy 2, and every one of my sampled guard, containment, launcher, pause and runs-root tests can fail for its fault. Of my 18 planted faults, 15 were caught by the tests that claim them (one is a control), and 3 were not: they show the three weak tests (Part 2.7).
- **What costs the most:**
  - two **nested reruns** (W1-05's switch-over case and W1-47's builder case). They re-run 5,400 cases the same close already runs, cost 14.6 minutes of every close's serial phase (47 % of it), and are why worksteal is deferred (DEC-892 a). Retiring them needs no code (P-I1-5);
  - the **W1-46 live host cases**, which fail on the network, not on load. They were among the findings of four refused closes, most recently W2-63's on 2026-10-21 (they passed in all three closes of today). DEC-892's new network check helps, but it probes from outside the sandbox, about an hour before the session runs (F-1).
- **What encodes old rules:**
  - 28 permanently skipped W1-01 cases and 8 record pins of the retired interim rules (P-I1-1);
  - a Read deny on `docs/source/`, a folder removed on 2026-10-01 (P-I1-2);
  - W1-28's DEC-385 check and the hook helper's redaction, whose basis Policy 2 removed (P-I1-3, P-I1-4);
  - five tests pinning the test designer's whole-folder write that DEC-769 item 3 limits, a decision W2-21's ticket file does not carry (F-4, P-I1-6).
  - The register entries Policy 2 replaced carry no "Changed by" mark, so a DEC-866 sweep cannot find the tests that cite them (F-3).
- **What fails open:**
  - one silent default: Policy 2 (c) for the test designer is written to `w2/integrate`, so in EXAMIN or the next wave it refuses nothing (F-12, P-I1-9: an entry criterion of W2-27);
  - one latent gap: a failed restore of an acceptance test that a regression labelled "reverted" would pass every test that covers containment (with that fault planted, 1,357 cases ran and none failed for it), because the one test that claims it accepts either label (F-6).
  - Both are feature-gated or latent: no round now.
- **For the plan.**
  - **W2-57's serial list:** add W2-58's five live cases (they launch real sessions in the parallel phase today); let W1-50's 0.3-second latency case run in parallel; drop the switch-over case.
  - **`load_sensitive`:** W1-46's 23 reasons should name the network. The two p95 cases fail whenever the machine is loaded, even alone at a load of about one per core, and passed every time alone on a quiet machine: they belong in the quiet serial phase (Part 2.6).
  - **W2-57's resource gate:** two wall-clock limits went red in my runs under load, a first for both: W1-02's 5-second bound (168 cases) and the guard's own 20-second deadline (F-14).
  - **W2-66's map:** W1-28 depends on every suite's support modules by a file scan.

**Terms used** (each defined once here).
- **The guard:** the PreToolUse hook (`template/governance/kernel/hooks/pretooluse.py` with `src/gov/guard/`) that allows or refuses every tool call of a session, by role and ticket.
- **Containment:** the PostToolUse check (`src/gov/guard/containment.py`) that compares the tree after a Bash call with a snapshot taken before it, restores an acceptance test an engineer changed, and records or flags other changes.
- **The launcher:** `gov launch <role> <ticket>` (`src/gov/launch/launcher.py`), which starts a worker session of the Claude Code CLI inside the operating-system **sandbox** (a write fence and a network allowlist the CLI enforces with `bwrap`).
- **`gov pause`:** the freeze the owner or the orchestrator sets; only the owner in person lifts it (DEC-409).
- **The runs root:** `~/gov-os-runs/<ticket>/`, the durable scratch folder a session may write (W2-50, DEC-728).
- **Policy 2** (DEC-820): reads are open except four things: (a) secrets, (b) the held-out qualification material's contents, (c) the counterpart's work on the same ticket, (d) the hidden half of an evaluation set. W2-58 built it (merged 2026-10-21, not yet closed).
- **A close:** `gov close`, which runs a ticket's tests, then every other test in parallel (**the regression**), then the **serial-only** cases alone (`tests/acceptance/serial-only.txt`). **`load_sensitive`**: an owner-only list of tests re-run alone when they fail in a close. **`accepted_reds`**: an owner-only list of known reds that do not refuse a close.
- **The rehearsal:** the orchestrator's nightly full run of 2026-10-21 17:49 on `dbd3cc53` (`log/nightly/20261021-1743/`, JUnit files for both phases). **The tool trials:** the orchestrator's five full parallel runs of 2026-10-22 00:22 to 06:09 on a clone at `4166d8d9` (DEC-889 item 13; `log/tooltrials/20261022-0017/`); pytest-randomly shuffled all five (TOOL-TRIALS report).
- **A planted fault:** a one-line change I made to the code in my clone, to see whether the test that claims to catch it fails; the file was restored after each run.
- **Live session:** a test that starts the real Claude Code CLI (`local_only` marker): it writes under `~/.claude/` and spends the owner's usage.

**What I read.**
- Every test file of the area at least by its function list; in full: the step-1 and step-2 files and every sampled function (Part 2), with their support modules where a verdict depended on them; the code each verdict needed (`src/gov/guard/decide.py`, `protected.py`, `reads.py`, `containment.py`, `runs.py`, `install.py`, `src/gov/launch/launcher.py`, `src/gov/pause/command.py`, the kernel hooks).
- Lens H's report (`docs/assessment/2026-10/H-test-strategy.md`) and its folder `/tmp/gov-os-assessment-H/` (its ever-failed list, duplicate list and scripts), read only.
- Every recorded close result: the 47 test findings of all `gov close` answers in the orchestrator's scratch log folder and on `record/w2` (my parser `scripts/close_findings.py`, which also reads each close's load-sensitive re-runs); the three closes of today (W2-50's, refused; W2-58's, refused; W2-62's, passed), in which every case of my area passed in both phases; the queue logs `close-queue-*`; the rehearsal; the tool trials and their report; the W2-58 rewrite record `tests/acceptance/W2-58/EARLIER_CASES.md`.
- Ticket files and WBS cards of W1-01 to W1-05, W1-28, W1-45 to W1-47, W1-50, W2-21, W2-50, W2-58, W2-65, W2-66, and the list rows they cite.

**What I ran, and where.** Every run that wrote anything ran in my folder `/tmp/gov-os-assessment-I1/`, in my clone `repo/` (`git clone --no-hardlinks`, at `4166d8d9`), through two scripts of mine (`scripts/run.sh` for pytest, `scripts/runcmd.sh` for other commands):
- an empty environment (`env -i`), `HOME` and `TMPDIR` in my folder, `PYTHONUSERBASE=/home/usain/.local` (the installed pytest 9.0.3 and pytest-xdist 3.8.0), the git identity by environment variables;
- a private `/tmp` (`bwrap --dev-bind / / --bind <mine>/faketmp /tmp`), so no fixed `/tmp` folder of the product was shared;
- the resource gate checked in the same command before every start (1-minute load below 20, `MemAvailable` at least 6,710,886 kB, neither `CLOSE-RUNNING` nor `NIGHTLY-RUNNING`), and the run **stopped at once if either lock appeared**; the load at start, end and peak is in each run's `.meta` file.

**Tools installed: none. Settings changed: none. Files written: only my folder and this report.**

**Limits of this review.**
- **Live sessions were not run.** W1-46's 36 live cases, W1-50's two live freeze cases and W2-58's five live cases start the real Claude Code CLI, which writes under `~/.claude/` (outside the two places I may write) and spends the owner's usage. Their verdicts rest on the recorded runs: every close result, the close's own re-runs alone (W2-53's sweep, W2-63's close), the rehearsal and the five tool trials. Each such row says so.
- **W1-05's switch-over case (874 s alone) was not re-run:** its verdict (duplicate) rests on its code and on the rehearsal's timing, not on its outcome.
- **The close lock held the machine from 09:52 to 11:40** (the queue's wait for quiet, then W2-50's close with its rebuild, collection, record checks and network check; the close was refused at 11:40 for reasons outside my area, and the lock was removed). My first rebuild, started at 09:36, was stopped by my runner when the lock appeared (09:52:47). Every loading run of mine ran after the lock was gone, and each run's start, end and peak load is in its `.meta` file. Closes held the machine again from 11:53 to 13:28, 13:35 to 13:57 and 15:11 to 16:44 (W2-58's and W2-62's). My batches stopped at each lock and resumed where they had stopped; only the two rebuilds were stopped in the middle of a run (09:52 and 11:53), the third ran whole (17:37 to 17:58). Every other session also ran in the windows between closes, so my runs met an outside 1-minute load of 13 to 20 at their start and up to 151 at peak; each run's load is in Parts 2.5 to 2.8.
- **Load from other sessions.** The other three lens-I auditors ran rebuilds and suites on the same machine. My runs record the 1-minute load at start, end and peak; a flaky verdict says the load each run met.
- **The git-path timing of F-10** ran outside my runners: read-only `git` in my clone, one short process of about two seconds, at outside loads of 112 and 75 after a wait for a lower load timed out (`logs/git-path-timing*.out`). I count it as a light read like my other `git` reads, not as a loading run; its figures are an upper bound for that reason.
- **A quiet machine only once.** My alone runs started at an outside load of 15 to 20, just under the gate's ceiling. A window below a load of 4 came only at 19:07, after my other runs. In it, the two p95 cases ran alone five times (two of W1-02's three planned runs got the window) and the read-latency probe twice.
- **The sample** is 109 functions, 6 % of each folder's remaining functions (seed 1022, drawn before any result was read, `logs/sample.tsv`). Its rate of findings is an estimate for the 1,503 functions neither reviewed in steps 1 and 2 nor sampled; the static scan covers all 2,042 for the shapes it looks for.
- **"Never failing" (lens H's 99 %)** is not a list of named tests that could be reviewed one by one. I treated it as the population the sample, the planted faults and the static scan draw on, because a test that never fails is questionable only if it cannot fail.
- **The `gov retrieve` questions** ran in my clone after its rebuild, so its query cache stayed in my folder; five questions, `--batch-size 8`, each stopped at its budget after two batches (`BUDGET_EXHAUSTED_WITH_GAPS`). It answered three: the decisions that replaced the read refusals (DEC-820, DEC-821, DEC-857 and DEC-873, with W2-58's card and change record), W1-46's `load_sensitive` entries (DEC-760, DEC-762, DEC-813, row W2-M364 and W2-57's card), and the second-name rule (`protected.py` and its W1-02 and W2-50 tests). It did not find DEC-769 for its question, nor DEC-889 item 11 or DEC-892 for worksteal and the nested runs, even after one continuation each (`--continue`, DEC-860; both ended `SATURATED`). DEC-892 is newer than my clone's head. I read those three in the register.
- **The code-graph tool** was not reachable as a tool from this session; code questions were answered by reading the code.
- **No GitHub access, no install, no setting changed.**
- **Refusals met: 4.**
  - (1) My `ls docs/source` was refused by the harness's permission deny rule on `docs/source/`. It is not one of Policy 2's four, and it is the rule P-I1-2 is about. Taken, not retried.
  - (2) A Bash command holding an inline script with backticks and nested quotes was refused by the guard: "the command cannot be read, so it is not known to be free of the lift form of gov pause (--off): refused (DEC-409)". Taken; the same change went through a script file.
  - (3) A call of the CLI's `Monitor` tool (to follow the close queue's log) was refused by the guard: "the tool 'Monitor' is not known to the guard: refused to every role (DEC-685)". Taken, not retried. It fails closed as DEC-685 intends and is a known row (W2-M016, "harmless tools (Monitor, Artifact, mcp__ read-only) refused in guarded sessions"); no new finding.
  - (4) The same refusal as (2), at 19:45, for an inline check script with backticks. Taken; the check went through a script file.
  - The harness also blocked one foreground `sleep` (not a refusal of the guard); waits were background until-loops.
  - No read refusal of Policy 2's kind (a search from the root, `governance/project/` or `.claude/`) was met. The hook source (`.rulesync/hooks.jsonc`) runs the tree's own `src/`, which holds W2-58's guard since its merge (2026-10-21). I did not open `.claude/settings.json`: it names the held-out path, which this brief tells me never to handle. The containment hook also reported, after several of my read-only calls, changes that the orchestrator made at the same moment (its commits and record files) as outside my paths, "left in place" or "flagged": the known rows W2-M020 and W2-L135. Nothing of mine was changed or reverted.

## 2. The verdict table

One row per test function reviewed (a function's parameter sets are its cases). Ids are relative to `tests/acceptance/` (or `tests/` for `unit/`). Every verdict rests on the evidence named in its row; the runs and planted faults behind them are in Parts 2.5 to 2.8.

### 2.1 Step 1: failing or questionable tests (load_sensitive, serial-only, failed in closes, the rehearsal or the trials, duplicates)

Functions here: 118 (77 valid and needed, 22 outdated, 9 flaky, 8 duplicate, 1 obsolete, 1 weak); cases: 283.

**Evidence keys used in this table:**

- **K-W02-E:** The hook-listing helper's redaction of deny paths inside hook commands. It exists so that 'no session needs to open the settings file' (DEC-525) and must hide deny values (DEC-548 helper redaction, DEC-553 home-folder spellings). Policy 2 opened reads of the settings file and of the held-out list's names (DEC-820 (b), DEC-821 item 3, W2-58 KPI 5): the redaction is now a requirement with no basis. Fails closed only; 7.3 s of case time in the rehearsal.
- **K-W02-F:** The helper's listing contract (events, matchers, commands; stated failures; writes nothing). The helper is still used by sessions (lens H's one listing) and W2-58 keeps a case that it lists the hook events.
- **K-1:** One real engineer session per run (module fixture); no recorded failure in any close or in W2-03's run (the 23 setup errors were the research fixture's); passed in the rehearsal's serial phase. The only proof that a launched worker is sandboxed for real (CAP-61.b, CAP-58.d, CAP-49.b; DEC-813: 'W1-46 proves the launcher and the sandbox'). Not re-run here.
- **K-2:** Same research fixture; same record.
- **K-3:** The secrets scan finishes on large stores, reuses a clean result by hash, stays whole and closed (DEC-853). Never seen failing.
- **K-4:** Each pins one distinct repair of W1-03's probe (dirty-path fingerprints, no restore of dirty paths, links judged where they lie, Write overlap, -z names, renames, no silent skip, non-forward moves, readable report, leftovers). Read: test_probe_repairs.py:224-470, 566ff.
- **K-5:** Builder twins of W1-45's subagent-scope acceptance cases (in-process decide vs the hook process; DEC-042, MR-3 by design: the engineer's own tests). Two share names with W1-45's.

### 2.2 Step 2: suites of closed tickets whose feature a later decision changed

Functions here: 303 (281 valid and needed, 14 obsolete, 7 outdated, 1 duplicate); cases: 3011.

**Evidence keys used in this table:**

- **K-6:** Same skip.
- **K-7:** They test the oracle (w1_01_support.acceptance_test_offenders, merge_offences) of the MR-3 history test above, both directions, in small repositories. Test code about test code: needed as long as that test stands.
- **K-W02-C:** Bounded decision time: an input past the guard's reading bound is refused within 5 s (BOUND_S, w1_02_round_support.py:127); within the bound it is decided (W2-58 round 3, TD-4 (b)). Wall-clock with a wide margin; these cases never failed, though a case with the same bound went red once under load in my runs (F-14). PF-4 (Part 2.7).
- **K-W02-A:** Rewritten by W2-58's test designer from 'refused' to Policy 2's answer (allowed, or refused for (c) where the engineer's search takes in its own existing acceptance folder); each file was run red on the base's guard before the change (EARLIER_CASES.md, rounds 1-4); green in the rehearsal and in my run. Distinctive value now: the remaining rules ((a), (b), (c), second names, bounds) must not misfire on these spellings. Overlaps W2-58's reads_open at KPI level. PF-1 (Part 2.7).
- **K-W02-B:** The second-name rule and its controls: a second name (move, link, linking copy, in-place edit with a backup) of the settings file, the held-out list, a copy or a holding folder is refused to every role, and the same forms on other folders are decided as before; wording no longer asked (W2-58 round 2, TD-2 (i)). Several names still say 'as a read', the rule's old basis; the rule is now held on the write side ('a writable alias', protected.py:1-16, 27-32). PF-2 (Part 2.7).
- **K-W02-D:** Writes to the two files, their copies and the user-level settings file are decided as before by the allow-list: Policy 2 widened no write rule (W2-58's failure KPI).
- **K-8:** DEC-385 (owner, 2026-10-05, stricter-only): 'every test fixture that copies the repository leaves governance/project/held-out.yaml out', because the list's content (the held-out paths) was then refused to read (DEC-508, DEC-525, DEC-548 copies). Policy 2 made the list's names readable (DEC-820 (b), DEC-821 item 3, W2-58 KPI 5) and W2-58 opened reads of copies (TD-2 (ii)). The check now enforces a rule with no basis, and it couples W1-28 to every suite: it discovers copying fixtures by reading every file under tests/acceptance and runs them (KNOWN_COPIERS: W1-05, W1-07, W1-33, W1-46, W1-47, W1-49), so a new whole-tree copy anywhere must take a root parameter and leave the file out or W1-28 goes red.
- **K-9:** The orchestrator's write scope: anywhere but tests/acceptance/** (DEC-156), out-of-scope changes recorded not flagged (DEC-171, DEC-177), .gov-runtime denied but scratch (DEC-176), subagent scope (DEC-178). No later decision changes these in-repository rules: DEC-728/DEC-743 (runs root), DEC-769 (leads sandboxed, W2-21), DEC-819 and DEC-887 (records) concern paths outside the repository or unbuilt tickets. Green in the rehearsal.
- **K-10:** Which folder a research ticket's one folder may be (never tests/acceptance, never .gov-runtime, under experiments/). DEC-827's experiments/EXP-00n/ layout lies under experiments/, so it is consistent.
- **K-11:** The launcher's held-out Read deny rules (one per configured path, read at each launch, refusal on a broken file, none without the file) and a scan that no ticket file names a held-out path. DEC-873 item 2: the held-out rules stay in the sandbox.
- **K-12:** The research session's Edit fence computed at launch, its exceptions (.git, entries with glob characters), and the guard's allow-list for paths made later. Note: the exception for glob-named entries rests on EXP-001's finding that the Linux sandbox skips them (CAP-61.c); P-W2-58-1 (DEC-873's basis) measured that Read rules with wildcards do bind on CLI 2.1.292; Edit rules are unmeasured (launcher.py _runtime_rules docstring).
- **K-13:** The strict, fail-closed sandbox block passed by one --settings value; no sandbox read from the repository; the CLI by absolute path; only worker roles launched. Untouched by DEC-821/DEC-873 (they concern Read rules for (c) and (d)).
- **K-14:** Policy 2 (b): a call that names or reaches the held-out path is refused by every route and role; a broken configuration fails closed; the exception for the two files that hold the path. W2-58 kept (b) unchanged and reads it the same way (its README, 'still refused'); DEC-873 item 2 keeps the held-out Read rules. These are the primary pins of (b).
- **K-15:** One option reader for copy-like commands (DEC-778): second names of the protected file refused however the options are written, plain copies and unprotected files allowed. Wording asks only 'refused, no path' since W2-58 round 3. Names still say 'as a read' (old basis); the rule is held on the write side (TD-2 (i)).

### 2.8 The static scan for shapes that cannot fail

`scripts/weak_scan.py` read every one of the area's 2,042 test functions for:
- no assertion and no asserting helper;
- an `assert … or …` that may accept either outcome;
- a bare `return` inside an `if` before the end;
- a `pytest.skip` inside the body.

It flagged 47 shapes. I read each:
- 22 "no assertion" hits call module helpers that assert (`_policy_2`, `_refused`, `_file_tools_and_bash`);
- 2 are "does not raise" checks (`_check_cli_args`), and 1 is a fixture;
- the `or` and early-return shapes either assert both acceptable outcomes named by their KPI (a refusal by the guard or a finding by containment) or check report wording;
- the skips are environment guards (running as root, no `perl`, a shallow clone without the judged commit, a scan that leaves no record to damage). None of them fired in the rehearsal: every case of these files passed.

What the scan leaves is two weak tests: `TestStopHook::test_no_ticket_returns_immediately` (no assertion) and `TestRepair10ActionTellsTheTruth::test_failed_restore_is_flagged` (accepts either label). The third weak test, `test_protected.py::test_the_brace_pattern_finds_what_the_one_it_replaced_found`, has an assertion the scan accepts; I found it by reading, and PF-5 confirmed it. There is also one vacuous case: W1-46's `test_another_role_gains_nothing_from_the_research_ticket` returns without asserting for the test designer. The scan also showed that the real restore-failure test, `test_restore_verify_and_timeout.py::…::test_blocked_restore_says_flagged_not_reverted`, guards only the other restore branch (PF-7b).

## 3. Counts per verdict, and what each class costs a close today

**Reviewed:** 540 test functions with 3,844 cases, of the area's 2,042 functions and 9,896 cases (26 % of the functions, 39 % of the cases):
- step 1: 118 functions;
- step 2: 303;
- the sample: 109;
- in passing: 10 (9 compared for findings, and 1 first seen red in my own baseline run).

Time is the case time each class took in the rehearsal of 2026-10-21 (JUnit, setup and teardown included), split by the phase it runs in:
- in the **serial phase** a case runs alone, so its case time is the close's wall time;
- in the **parallel phase** about 20 workers share the work, so its wall cost is roughly a twentieth, except that a case far longer than its neighbours lengthens the tail;
- a lead's verification runs every suite serially, so there the whole case time counts.

| Verdict | Functions | Cases | Parallel-phase case time | Serial-phase case time (= close wall time) | Lead verification (serial, all of it) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| valid and needed | 465 | 3,555 | 1,323.1 s | 46.8 s | 1,369.9 s |
| duplicate | 12 | 88 | 280.7 s | 874.1 s | 1,154.8 s |
| flaky | 11 | 54 | 4.3 s | 48.3 s | 52.6 s |
| outdated | 34 | 102 | 17.4 s | 0.0 s | 17.4 s |
| obsolete | 15 | 42 | 0.4 s | 0.0 s | 0.4 s |
| weak | 3 | 3 | 0.4 s | 0.0 s | 0.4 s |
| not reproducible | 0 | 0 | 0 s | 0 s | 0 s |
| **Reviewed in all** | **540** | **3,844** | **1,626.3 s** | **969.2 s** | **2,595.5 s** |

**What the classes cost and would save.**
- **Duplicates: 874.1 s of serial wall time in every close.** W1-05's switch-over case alone is 874.1 s, 47 % of the rehearsal's whole serial phase (1,842.6 s), so retiring it shortens every close by about 14.6 minutes.
  - In the parallel phase the duplicates hold 280.7 s of case time. Most of it is two cases that sit on the run's tail: W2-50's large-store case (169.3 s) and W1-47's builder rerun (78.2 s; 300 s when it timed out under worksteal).
  - Each lead verification spends 1,154.8 s on them.
  - They also keep worksteal out of every run (DEC-892 a).
- **Flaky: 48.3 s in the serial phase and 4.3 s in the parallel phase** (the latter are W1-02's path-limit case and W1-50's trailer case, both first seen red in my runs, F-14). Their cost is not their time but the refusals:
  - W1-05's p95 case was among the findings of two Wave 1 closes (W1-02 R8, W1-32) and of W2-01's;
  - W1-46's host cases were among the findings of four refused closes (W1-15 H2, W1-31 b2, W2-53's sweep, W2-63's on 2026-10-21), each a whole close to repeat (W2-63's took 74 minutes of a quiet window);
  - W1-02's p95 case went red in the verifications of 11 tickets' leads, each followed by a re-run alone.
- **Outdated, obsolete and weak: 18.2 s together,** negligible in time. Their cost is wrong guidance and coupling:
  - 28 skips counted in every close record;
  - a refusal of a folder that no longer exists;
  - W1-28's check, which every new copying fixture of any suite must satisfy;
  - three tests that cannot fail for the fault they name; for the restore label, none of the 1,357 cases that cover containment caught the fault either (F-6).
- **Valid and needed: 1,323.1 s parallel, 46.8 s serial.** The serial part is the W1-46 engineer session, W1-50's two live freeze cases and the W1-50 latency case. The latency case is 0.3 s and can move to the parallel phase (Part 4.2).

**The area's weight in a close** (rehearsal):
- 9,842 of 15,499 parallel cases (63.5 %) but 4,251.7 of 13,417.4 s of parallel case time (31.7 %);
- 54 of 263 serial cases with 969.2 of 1,842.6 s (52.6 %), most of it the one duplicate.

## 4. What it changes

### 4.1 For W2-66's path-to-suite map

W2-66 selects "the ticket's own suite, the unit tests, and the suites of every ticket whose allowed paths overlap the change" (DEC-880 item 2), widened by the shared core and by test-side imports (DEC-889 item 1). Its selection lists move into their own file, a test map owned by W2-66 and W2-68, and the path map stays a shared record no ticket names (DEC-896 item 2, 2026-10-22): what follows is for that test map. My area's tickets name these paths (their ticket files):

| Suite | Its ticket's allowed paths | What its tests reach that those paths do not name |
|---|---|---|
| W1-28 (`gov pause`) | `src/gov/pause/**`, `src/gov/launch/**`, their unit folders | `test_w1_28_fixture_copies.py` **reads every file under `tests/acceptance/`** to find whole-tree copying fixtures and **runs them** (today W1-05's, W1-07's, W1-33's, W1-46's, W1-47's and W1-49's support modules). An import graph cannot see this (it is a file scan, not an import). A change to any suite's support module that copies the tree can turn W1-28 red. If DEC-385 is retired (P-I1-4) the dependency goes with it |
| W1-05 (switch-over) | `.claude/settings.json`, `governance/project/bootstrap.md`, `roster.yaml`, `.claude/agents/**` | `test_the_dependencies_pass_their_acceptance_tests_at_the_switch_over` runs the whole W1-02, W1-03 and W1-04 suites in a child. Its live-hook cases run the kernel hooks and the guard (shared core). If the switch-over case is retired (P-I1-5) the nested dependency goes |
| W1-47 (guard hardening) | `src/gov/guard/**`, kernel hooks and settings, `.claude/settings.json`, `held-out.yaml`, unit folders | `test_the_builder_tests_of_the_guard_pass` runs `tests/unit/guard` in a child: already always selected by W2-66's rule, so a duplicate, not a gap (P-I1-5) |
| W1-01 (interim guardrails) | `.claude/settings.json`, `governance/project/bootstrap.md` | `test_only_the_test_designer_commits_to_acceptance_tests` reads **the whole history of `tests/acceptance/`** (MR-3). Under the declared map it runs only when the settings file or the bootstrap record changes, so the one whole-history check of MR-3 would rarely run in a scoped close. The close's own commit judgement (W1-50's `judge_commits`, DEC-453) covers the ticket's own commits; this test is the backstop for history: keep it in the nightly and in every full run |
| W1-46, W1-50, W2-58 (live cases) | `src/gov/launch/**` and others | The installed Claude Code CLI (`~/.local/bin/claude`, its pinned version in the tool registry) and the network. A CLI upgrade changes their outcome with no repository path changed: select them on a change of the CLI's pin, or keep them in the nightly (DEC-889 item 2 moves live tests there) |
| W1-50 (containment by trailers, freeze) | containment, `decide*`, pause, launch, unit folders | Test-side imports of `w1_46_support` (launch), `w1_28_support` (pause), `w1_02_support` and `w1_03_support` (lens H finding 3); W2-66's test-side import rule (DEC-889 item 1) covers them |

The guard (`src/gov/guard/**`) and the kernel hooks are shared core (DEC-880 item 2 (a)): any change there runs everything, so most of this area is always selected by a guard change. `src/gov/launch/**` and `src/gov/pause/**` are not shared core; W1-28, W1-46, W1-50, W2-50 and W2-58 all name `src/gov/launch/**`, so the declared map selects them together.

**Record-reading tests (DEC-889 item 2, the set that runs on every head).** Only one test of my area reads a record-only path (DEC-891's list: `.tickets/`, `docs/checkpoints/`, `docs/close/`, `docs/probes/`, the register, the plan files, `governance/project/orchestration/`): W1-01's `test_implementer_commits_stay_inside_allowed_paths`, which reads today's ticket files. A record-only commit that edits an old W1 ticket's `allowed_paths` turns it red while the code fingerprint stays the same. I propose retiring it (obsolete, P-I1-1); if it stays, it belongs in the record-reading set. Every other repository-state test of the area reads code-fingerprinted paths (`.claude/`, `governance/project/path-map.yaml`, `held-out.yaml`, git history of `tests/`), so DEC-883's reuse does not hide them.

Not on `load_sensitive`, so a setup failure under load refuses the close outright (finding F-2) |

Nothing in it needs the machine to itself (Part 2.6) |

Alone, with an outside 1-minute load of 15 to 20 at the start (about one per core), W1-02's case failed in 2 of 3 runs (1 and 4 of its 5 cases) and W1-05's in 3 of 3 (7, 4 and 4 of 9). Inside their own suites' worksteal runs, W1-02's failed in 1 of 3 and W1-05's in 3 of 3 (9, 6 and 6 of 9). Alone on a quiet machine (load below 4) both passed every time: W1-02's in 2 runs, W1-05's in 3. They also passed in the quiet serial phases of the rehearsal and of today's three closes; W1-05's failed in W2-01's serial phase while leads were running (2026-10-17). The serial phase, kept quiet, is the one place they can pass: keep them there (Part 2.6) |

### 4.3 For the `load_sensitive` and `accepted_reds` lists

- **`accepted_reds`:** no entry of my area. (W1-42's two reds belong to area 3 or 4.)
- **`load_sensitive`, W1-02's and W1-05's p95 cases (2 entries, 14 cases):** still justified, and my runs name the cause: machine load, whoever makes it. Alone, W1-05's case failed at a 1-minute load of 15 to 20 at its start and passed every time below 4. So the re-run alone cures it only while the close's quiet window holds (DEC-780). A lead's verification runs beside other work, where it cannot pass (Part 2.6). Their fix row, W2-M332, is a Wave 3 row with no date; DEC-890 gives such rows a target date. Fix ticket: W2-57 (the close's resource gate or an isolated timing suite, DEC-823 (b)).
- **W1-02's 168 cases with the 5-second bound, and W1-50's trailer case that met the guard's 20-second deadline (F-14):** on neither list. Each went red in one of my runs of it, none in the record. I propose no entry: the fix is the close's resource gate and the bound's measure (W2-57), and an entry would hide the next red of the same kind.
- **`load_sensitive`, W1-46's 23 entries:** still justified by the record (W2-53's sweep close: 23 failed in the run, 20 passed alone), but **their reason text is stale and partly wrong**:
  - it says "fails at setup under parallel load", yet the file has been on the serial-only list since before these entries, so in a close they run alone, after the parallel phase. The load that broke them in a close (W2-53's sweep, 2026-10-18) came from other sessions running beside it, which DEC-780's quiet window now keeps out;
  - the latest failure (DAEO-pl6k, 2026-10-21) failed in the run **and alone**: a network cause the re-run cannot cure, and it refused the close.
  - Fix ticket: W2-57 (DEC-813), with DEC-892 (3)'s network check and DEC-889 item 2 (live tests to the nightly). When the cases move to the nightly, the 23 entries leave the list (it may only shrink, DEC-766).

## 5. Findings

Each finding carries the two lines of the judgement rules (JUDGEMENT.md, DEC-808). Only "yes" to the first question together with "fails open" or "loses work" fails the audit's item and justifies a round; a feature-gated risk is an entry criterion of the ticket that switches the feature on (DEC-803); a "no" on the first question is a row for the Wave 3 hardening pass (DEC-801).

### F-1. W2-63's close was refused by a W1-46 live case that failed on the network, in the run and again alone

- **Evidence.**
  - `close-queue-20261021-2241/close-DAEO-pl6k.json` (W2-63's close, 2026-10-21 22:57 to 00:11, exit 3): "the load-sensitive test `…test_the_sandbox_accepts_a_connection_to_a_starting_host_of_the_research_allowlist[static.crates.io]` failed in the run and failed alone too", one of its eight findings.
  - The 17 host cases ask a real research session to `curl -m 20` each of 17 outside hosts through the sandbox's proxy (`test_w1_46_live_sessions.py:290-292, 345-357`). Recorded failures: `developer.mozilla.org` (W1-15 H2, 2026-10-10), `static.crates.io` (W1-31 b2, 2026-10-13), `files.pythonhosted.org`, `objects.githubusercontent.com`, `pypi.org` (W2-53's sweep, 2026-10-18, each failing again alone), `static.crates.io` (2026-10-21). Six failures in four closes, each of which was refused with it among its findings. Four of the six failed again in the close's re-run alone (W2-53's sweep and W2-63's close); the two in Wave 1 came before the re-run existed (DEC-757).
  - Their `load_sensitive` reason says "fails at setup under parallel load"; a failure alone is not load, and the re-run alone cannot cure it.
  - The close keeps no failure message (`--tb=line`), so the exact error (DNS, TLS, proxy, timeout) is not recorded anywhere (lens H's localisation finding).
  - **Already decided in part:** DEC-892 (3) (owner, 2026-10-22 09:48) adds a check before each close that the live tests' hosts are reachable; DEC-889 item 2 moves live tests to the nightly.
  - The check as built (`tools/net_check.py`, gate (g), first run 10:23 today: "17 hosts checked, 0 unreachable") curls each host directly from the machine, not through the sandbox's proxy the session uses, and well before the session runs: in W2-50's close today the check ran at 10:23:03, the close started at 10:28:38 and its serial phase at about 11:04, with W1-05's 874-second switch-over ahead of W1-46's file (my read-only `ps` of the close's processes at 11:11). All 17 host cases passed in that close, and in W2-58's and W2-62's later today.
- **Ordinary work: yes,** because every close runs the research session against 17 live hosts.
- **Consequence: other** (fails closed: a refused close of about 74 minutes of a quiet window, and an iteration counted against a ticket that did nothing wrong).
- **Severity:** high (it refused W2-63's close on 2026-10-21). What remains after DEC-892: the pre-close check should reach the hosts by the route the session uses (the sandbox's proxy, from inside a `bwrap` sandbox), or it can pass while the case still fails; and the entries' reason text should say "network", so the next reader does not treat it as load (W2-57).

### F-2. W2-58's live sessions are missing from the serial-only list, so every close starts real sessions in its parallel phase

- **Evidence.** `tests/acceptance/W2-58/test_w2_58_launched_sessions.py`: four functions (five cases) marked `local_only` start real engineer and test-designer sessions. The file is not in `serial-only.txt` (`grep W2-58` finds nothing). The rehearsal's `parallel.xml` and all five tool-trial runs show them inside the parallel phase (12.6 s and 10.1 s for the two sessions). They are not on `load_sensitive`. W1-46's same kind of session failed at setup under load (W2-03 run 2: 23 setup errors; DEC-813: again at load about 25).
- **Ordinary work: yes,** because every close and every lead verification runs them.
- **Consequence: other** (fails closed: a setup failure under load refuses the close with no re-run; each run also spends the owner's usage).
- **Severity:** medium. Fix: W2-57's list (kind `live-session`), or the nightly with the other live cases (DEC-889 item 2).

### F-3. The read-rule decisions Policy 2 replaced carry no "Changed by" mark, so a DEC-866 sweep cannot find the tests that cite them

- **Evidence.**
  - DEC-820 says it "Replaces: the listing refusals on `governance/` and `.claude/` (DEC-508), and every other read restriction beyond the four". DEC-821 item 3 narrows DEC-525's and DEC-557's refusals. DEC-873 changes DEC-821's sandbox clause.
  - The status lines of DEC-508, DEC-525, DEC-548, DEC-553, DEC-557, DEC-573, DEC-575 (the read rules on the two protected files and root searches) and DEC-385 (fixtures leave the held-out list out) say ACCEPTED with no "Changed by" (register at `8d625805`). DEC-723's status line does not show DEC-820's replacement of its listing refusal either. (DEC-562, DEC-570 and DEC-574 stay in force: the bounded reading time, the NUL refusal and the held-out check's length rule, all kept by W2-58.) DEC-821's own line carries DEC-873's mark, so the convention exists.
  - In my area 24 files (18 test files, 6 support modules) cite DEC-508, DEC-525, DEC-548, DEC-553, DEC-557, DEC-573 or DEC-575 (my `grep`, `logs/cite-files.txt`), and 10 function names still say "refused as a read" for a rule now held on the write side (`protected.py:1-16`).
  - Two tests rest on such an unmarked entry and are outdated (W1-28's DEC-385 check, P-I1-4; the hook helper's redaction, P-I1-3), and one more rests on a read rule DEC-820 replaced without naming it (the `docs/source` deny, P-I1-2).
  - **A status-line sweep finds none of them.** My DEC-866-style check (`scripts/cited_status.py`) took the 170 decisions the area's test and support files cite.
    - Five carry a mark: DEC-120, DEC-183, DEC-801, DEC-821, DEC-880. In each, the tests rest on the part that stands.
    - Every stale rule this review found rests on an entry with no mark at all: DEC-385, DEC-508, DEC-525, DEC-548, DEC-553, DEC-557, and DEC-769's unbuilt item 3 (F-4).
- **Ordinary work: yes,** because designers and engineers read register entries and test names literally (DEC-866's premise).
- **Consequence: other** (tests that look current but rest on replaced rules; nothing opens).
- **Severity:** medium. Fix: the orchestrator marks the status lines (DEC-825 item 4's practice), delegated; the renames ride with the next guard test revision (DEC-802).

### F-4. DEC-769 item 3 (the test designer writes only its own acceptance folder) is not in W2-21's ticket file or paths, and five tests pin the old rule

- **Evidence.**
  - DEC-769 item 3 (owner, 2026-10-18): "The independent test designer writes only its own ticket's acceptance folder (`tests/acceptance/<its WBS id>/`)". Item 1 folds it into W2-21. W2-21's card carries it (WBS line 498); its ticket file `DAEO-1ahb.md` does not (no KPI line, `DEC-769` not in `sources`), and its allowed paths do not hold the guard (`src/gov/launch/**` only).
  - The guard still grants the designer all of `tests/acceptance/**` (`decide.py:153-154`).
  - Tests that pin the old rule:
    - W1-02 `test_test_designer_can_write_under_acceptance_tests` (4 of its 12 cases: another ticket's folder and `tests/acceptance/conftest.py`);
    - W1-02 `test_the_test_designer_s_multiedit_is_allowed_under_the_acceptance_tests_only[a-new-acceptance-test-of-another-ticket]`;
    - W1-03 `test_the_test_designer_s_changes_stay` (it writes another ticket's folder `W1-77` and expects silence) and `test_the_test_designer_s_scope_does_not_depend_on_the_ticket_paths` (a designer on ticket W1-95 writes W1-90's test and expects silence), `test_w1_03_test_independence.py:121-148`;
    - W1-46 `test_w1_46_protected_trees.py`'s reading ("the independent test designer writes `tests/acceptance/**`").
  - **A tension the decision does not settle:** test designers revise earlier tickets' tests by rule. W2-58's designer rewrote W1-02's, W1-50's and W2-50's tests (EARLIER_CASES.md), and DEC-874 and DEC-875 ordered such revisions. Under item 3 as written, those writes would be refused.
- **Ordinary work: yes,** because a designer writes in another ticket's folder in every revision round.
- **Consequence: fails open** against DEC-769 item 3 (today a designer may write any ticket's tests), but feature-gated: it exists until W2-21 builds the rule. So it is **an entry criterion of W2-21** (DEC-803), not a round.
- **Severity:** medium. Needs the owner's reading of "its own folder" for revision tickets (P-I1-6).

### F-5. Two nested reruns duplicate whole suites inside every close, cost 15 minutes of each close, and block worksteal

- **Evidence.**
  - W1-05's switch-over case runs W1-02, W1-03 and W1-04 again in a child: 874.1 s alone in the rehearsal's serial phase, 47 % of that phase's case time (1,842.6 s). In W2-50's close today (serial phase 1,769.9 s) its child pytest started about 34 s into the serial phase (one process, no `-n`); at 11:20:41, while it ran, the machine's 1-minute load was 0.8: the quiet window held the whole machine for one core's work.
  - W1-47's builder case runs `tests/unit/guard` again: 78.2 s in the rehearsal, 43 to 53 s in four trial runs, **300 s and a timeout** in the worksteal trial (`b2-worksteal.txt:280-320`). In my clone it passed every time: alone in 149.0, 58.4 and 65.8 s (the 149 s at an outside peak load of 98), and inside W1-47's own worksteal run in 109.4, 131.6 and 45.2 s. That is at most 44 % of its 300 s limit, so the trial's timeout needed the whole estate's worksteal run around it.
  - DEC-892 a defers worksteal (DEC-889 item 11 a, a measured 9 % saving) "until W2-67 or W2-57 removes the nested runs". W2-67 has no ticket file and waits for W2-66.
- **Ordinary work: yes,** because every close runs both.
- **Consequence: other** (14.6 minutes of each close's quiet window; a red under worksteal).
- **Severity:** medium-high for cost. Retiring the two cases (owner, P-I1-5) needs no new code.

### F-6. Three unit tests cannot fail for the fault they claim to catch

- **Evidence** (planted faults, Part 2.7):
  - `test_probe_repairs.py::TestRepair10ActionTellsTheTruth::test_failed_restore_is_flagged` accepts `"flagged" in actions or "reverted" in actions` (line 465), the very mislabel it is named for. Worse, its acceptance test is already dirty when the snapshot is taken (line 439 writes it before the pre-hook runs), so containment never tries to restore it (repair 2: "already dirty, not restored", `containment.py:1380-1389`) and labels it "flagged" for that reason. The restore-failure branch the test is named for (`containment.py:1399-1403`) is never reached; its chmod of the file (line 450) would not stop git anyway while the folder stays writable. With a failed restore from HEAD relabelled "reverted" (PF-7), it still passed. With the same mislabel on the other restore branch (PF-7b), `test_restore_verify_and_timeout.py::…::test_blocked_restore_says_flagged_not_reverted` failed: only that branch is guarded.
  - `test_w29_hooks.py::TestStopHook::test_no_ticket_returns_immediately` has no assertion. With the hook's early return removed (PF-6), it still passed.
  - `test_protected.py::test_the_brace_pattern_finds_what_the_one_it_replaced_found` compares two regex literals declared in the test. With the module's pattern reverted to the one DEC-570 replaced (PF-5), it still passed.
- **Ordinary work:**
  - for the restore label: **yes,** because a restore fails when another git command holds the index lock, which happens with parallel sessions in one worktree;
  - for the other two: no (a refactor of a regex; a Stop hook without a ticket).
- **Consequence:**
  - the restore label **fails open, latently.** Today's code labels a failed restore "flagged" correctly, but a regression that labels it "reverted" would tell the agent and the record that an engineer's change to an acceptance test was undone when it was not, and no test that covers containment notices it (with PF-7 in place, the 1,357 cases of `tests/unit/containment/`, `tests/unit/guard/test_records.py`, W1-03, W1-45, W1-50 and W1-05's live-hook file ran and none failed for it; their only four reds were W1-05's p95 cases, timing reds under the run's load);
  - the other two: other.
- **Severity:** medium for the first (a test revision, riding with the next containment ticket, DEC-802); low for the others.

### F-7. W1-50's live freeze cases do not carry DEC-784's environment fix

- **Evidence.**
  - DEC-784 (owner): W1-46's live cases failed when run from a Claude session because `CLAUDE_CODE_*` variables reached the launched session, and passed 36 of 36 with every `CLAUDE*` variable removed.
  - W1-50's two live freeze cases launch through the same `gov launch` path but remove a fixed list of six names (`test_w1_50_freeze_live_pause.py:53-54, 230`; `test_w1_50_freeze_launcher.py:173-174, 225`).
  - Not seen failing yet: they passed in the rehearsal's serial phase and in today's three closes.
- **Ordinary work: yes,** because closes run from the orchestrator's Claude session.
- **Consequence: other** (fails closed: a refused close if a newer `CLAUDE_CODE_*` variable breaks the launched session).
- **Severity:** low-medium. Fix: W2-57, with W1-46's fixture work (Rewrite-Reason: DEC-784).

### F-8. Retired and replaced rules still tested: 28 skipped cases, a removed folder's deny rule, DEC-385's fixture check, the helper's redaction

- **Evidence:**
  - W1-01's 28 cases skip in every run ("W1-05 is closed: the interim guardrail is retired (DEC-084)");
  - W1-01's history and record pins check frozen Wave 1 history;
  - W1-01's `docs/source` case asserts a Read deny on a folder removed from the tree on 2026-10-01 (my own `ls docs/source` was refused by that rule);
  - W1-28's 18 DEC-385 cases enforce a rule whose basis Policy 2 removed, and they couple W1-28 to every suite;
  - W1-02's 22 redaction cases enforce DEC-525/548/553's helper requirement, whose basis Policy 2 removed.
  - Parts 2 and 6 give each test and its package.
- **Ordinary work: yes,** because a designer adding a whole-tree copy must satisfy W1-28's check, and an agent meets the `docs/source` refusal.
- **Consequence: other** (fails closed; noise and coupling).
- **Severity:** low. Packages P-I1-1 to P-I1-4.

### F-9. CAP-61.c's premise that the Linux sandbox skips glob-named entries is measured wrong for Read rules and unmeasured for Edit rules

- **Evidence.**
  - CAP-61.c (v4.2) and W1-46's `test_an_entry_with_a_glob_character_in_its_name_is_left_out_of_the_list` rest on EXP-001: entries whose names hold `*`, `?` or `[` are skipped by the sandbox.
  - P-W2-58-1 (DEC-873's basis) measured on CLI 2.1.292 that Read rules with wildcards do become OS-level denials.
  - `launcher.py`'s `_runtime_rules` docstring says the same is "not measured" for Edit rules.
- **Ordinary work: no,** because a file whose name holds a glob character beside a research session's folder is rare.
- **Consequence: fails open** in principle: an opaque Bash write by a research session to such a file is not fenced.
- **Severity:** low. A row for W2-40 (EXP-002, the untested sandbox cases): re-measure Edit rules with wildcards on the pinned CLI.

### F-10. The 100 ms budget's tests do not time the slowest new read path (measured here: it adds tens of milliseconds at most)

- **Evidence.**
  - DEC-149: the 100 ms p95 budget covers "the guard's decision, for every tool".
  - W1-02's p95 case times five Write decisions; W1-05's times nine reads and writes by an engineer or a session with no role.
  - Under Policy 2 (c), a test designer's every read runs three `git` commands to find its branch's changed files (`reads.py:274-290`, `_changed`). No p95 case times a test designer's read.
  - My probes put a size on it. In W2-58's own fixture world on a quiet machine (19:09, load below 4; `scripts/td_read_timing.py`, 3 warm-up and 40 timed decisions per call), a test designer's read through the git path took 183 ms at the median and 192 ms at p95, against 182 and 193 ms for an auditor's plain read. Both figures are inflated by the fixture's uncompiled imports (`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`), so they are not the hook's production latency. The path's three git commands alone, in my full-size clone against real ticket branches with 48 to 318 changed files, took 11 to 25 ms at the median and at most 50 ms at p95, even at a 1-minute load of 75 (`scripts/git_path_timing.py`, 20 rounds each). So the path adds tens of milliseconds at most, against a budget of 100.
- **Ordinary work: yes,** because every read of a test designer takes that path.
- **Consequence: other** (a latency no test holds; my measurement finds it small).
- **Severity:** low. A row for W2-57's timing suite: one p95 case for a test designer's read on a branch with changes.

### F-11. Duplicates that cost the two slowest cases of the estate

- **Evidence.**
  - W2-50's `test_a_large_clean_store_is_decided_green` (169.3 s) asserts nothing that `test_a_large_store_among_many_small_files_is_decided_by_gov_check` (169.7 s) does not (the same 160 MiB store at the same throttled rate, inside the larger case, `test_w2_50_secrets_scan_scale.py:164-218`).
  - The time comes from the check's real 60-second limit. A declared short limit would show the same property in seconds (also for the 62-second hang case).
  - Smaller duplicates: five unit containment cases (`test_probe_repairs.py`'s `TestDEC132Defaults`, `TestRepair4`), one unit install case, W1-01's repository-merge case, W2-58's two "still refused" held-out functions.
- **Ordinary work: yes. Consequence: other** (time).
- **Severity:** low-medium. W2-67 (shrink), P-I1-7 (retire the small duplicates) and P-I1-1 (W1-01's merge case).

### F-12. Policy 2 (c) for the test designer names `w2/integrate` in code: in any repository whose integration branch has another name it silently refuses nothing

- **Evidence.**
  - `src/gov/guard/reads.py:36`: `BASE_BRANCH = "w2/integrate"  # DEC-826 Q2`. `_changed()` (lines 274-290) returns nothing when that branch does not exist, so the test designer's protected part is empty and every read of the ticket's implementation is allowed.
  - W2-58's `test_with_no_base_branch_nothing_is_refused_under_this_item` (4 cases) pins exactly that.
  - Nothing else in `src/gov/` names the branch.
  - EXAMIN's adoption tickets (W2-27, W2-29) say nothing of an integration branch. Wave 3's integration branch will not be called `w2/integrate` unless someone decides it.
- **Ordinary work: yes,** because a test designer launched in EXAMIN (Wave 2's adoption) or in Wave 3 reads its ticket's code as an ordinary read.
- **Consequence: fails open** (DEC-820 (c): "the test designer never reads that ticket's implementation"), silently: no refusal, no record.
- **Severity:** medium-high, but **feature-gated** (it exists only once a repository runs without `w2/integrate`), so it is an **entry criterion** of W2-27 and W2-29 and of Wave 3's start (DEC-803), not a round now. Fix: the base branch as a path-map setting read by the guard, refused (fail closed) when missing in a repository that declares Policy 2 (c); the case revised with it.

### F-13. Stale names and constants (rows)

- `test_only_the_four_worker_roles_are_launched` (five worker roles since DEC-386).
- W1-50's latency bound uses `HOOK_TIMEOUT_S = 30` (DEC-580 set the hook entries' limit to 60 s; stricter, harmless).
- `test_w1_02_protected_reads.py`'s docstring names a renamed case.
- The second-name functions named "as a read".
- `protected.py`'s refusal cites "DEC-821 item 4" for a write-side rule; DEC-821 item 4 is the read-side failure KPI, and the write basis is DEC-820 item 4.
- The root acceptance conftest's session tripwire fails a whole test session if the real `~/.local/state/gov-os/` mirror changes during it. A real `gov pause` during a close would therefore refuse that close (`tests/acceptance/conftest.py:31-74`).
- **Ordinary work:**
  - the names: yes;
  - the tripwire: no, because a real pause during a close is rare.
- **Consequence: other.**
- **Severity:** low. Rows for the next revision of each suite.

### F-14. Two wall-clock limits break under load in the parallel phase: W1-02's 5-second bound and the guard's own 20-second deadline

- **Evidence.**
  - **The 5-second bound.** W1-02's "answer in bounded time" (DEC-562) is checked as the hook process's wall time, start to exit, against `BOUND_S = 5.0` ("Generous: an ordinary decision takes well under a second on a busy machine; this is not the latency KPI", `w1_02_round_support.py:124-127`). 29 functions with 168 cases in six files ask for it (my `scripts/bound_cases.py`). All run in the parallel phase; none is on the serial list or `load_sensitive`.
    - In the first of my three W1-02 worksteal runs (14:52 to 14:56; 1-minute load 18 at the start, peak 41), `test_a_path_field_longer_than_the_guard_resolves_stays_refused_with_a_reason_of_its_own[the-path-of-a-Glob-just-over-the-limit]` failed: "the hook took 5.5 s (the bound is 5 s)". It took 0.05 s in the rehearsal. It passed in the other two worksteal runs and in three runs alone (at most 0.9 s).
  - **The guard's deadline.** The guard denies any call it cannot decide within 20 seconds of its start (DEC-580, `pretooluse.py:16-41`); for a Bash call that time also holds containment's snapshot (`git status` and `HEAD`, DEC-126).
    - In my area baseline (18:04 to 18:27, `-n auto`, peak load 52), W1-50's `test_a_commit_without_trailers_is_judged_against_the_caller[engineer-inside-its-ticket-paths]` failed because the guard reached that deadline on its fixture's call and refused it. The case took 20.4 s; at most 0.5 s in the rehearsal.
    - It passed in my three W1-50 worksteal runs (peaks 15, 77 and 70) and in three runs alone. Every W1-03 and W1-50 case that runs a call through `assert_let_through` can meet the same deadline.
  - No earlier failure of either kind is on record (the close findings, lens H's extracts). Both are timing, not the guard: the guard failed closed, as DEC-580 intends.
- **Ordinary work: yes,** because every close and every lead verification runs these cases in the parallel phase, and the machine reached loads of 40 to 150 today while closes, leads and the four lens-I auditors ran.
- **Consequence: other** (fails closed: a refused close, or a red lead verification, with nothing wrong in the guard). For real sessions the same deadline refuses an agent's call under such load, which the agent may retry; nothing is lost.
- **Severity:** low (first seen, at loads two to three times the machine's 20 cores). A row for W2-57:
  - the close's resource gate keeps the load below these limits;
  - the 5-second bound measures the hook's CPU time rather than its wall time, or its cases move to the timing suite (DEC-823 (b)).

## 6. Owner packages

None of these packages blocks this audit; each blocks only the change it proposes. No test was changed by this review (DEC-889 item 16 d): revisions go to independent test designers with a `Rewrite-Reason:`, and every retirement is the owner's.

### P-I1-1. Retire W1-01's interim-era tests and W1-05's retirement record pin (obsolete)

1. **Context.**
   - W1-01 built the interim guardrails of the bootstrap: settings deny rules, an operator diff procedure and an install rule, valid "until W1-05" (DEC-084).
   - W1-05 switched the repository to the live guard and retired them.
   - W1-01's tests were written to skip the interim checks once W1-05 closed.
2. **What happened.**
   - 28 cases of W1-01 skip in every run with "W1-05 is closed: the interim guardrail is retired (DEC-084)" (rehearsal; my run). They are 7 functions: `test_implementer_write_to_acceptance_tests_is_denied`, `test_acceptance_test_deny_covers_every_depth`, `test_install_command_is_denied`, `test_pipe_to_shell_and_sudo_are_denied`, `test_guardrails_leave_ordinary_implementer_work_alone`, `test_interim_rule_stays_in_force_once_introduced`, `test_no_install_is_recorded_before_w1_05`.
   - Eight more pass but check only frozen Wave 1 history or the text of its record:
     - `test_a_dependency_is_read_in_either_form_of_the_id` tests a helper of the test file used only by a skipped case;
     - `test_implementer_commits_stay_inside_allowed_paths` checks commits up to W1-05 against today's ticket files;
     - five `test_w1_01_bootstrap_record.py` cases pin sections of `bootstrap.md`;
     - W1-05's `test_the_operator_diff_check_is_recorded_as_retired`.
   - `test_the_integration_merges_of_this_repository_pass` duplicates `test_only_the_test_designer_commits_to_acceptance_tests` (same oracle, same merges).
   - Total: 16 functions, 43 cases, about 6 s per run, plus 28 skips reported in every close record.
3. **Why it matters.**
   - They cost little time, but each skip is counted in every close's `skipped` total, which nobody reads by name (lens H, A5).
   - The record pins make a reorganisation of `bootstrap.md` a red close.
   - The history in git is the record and stays whatever happens to these tests.
4. **Options.**
   - (a) Retire the 16 functions now, in one test-designer commit with `Rewrite-Reason: retired by the owner (P-I1-1)`. Keep `test_only_the_test_designer_commits_to_acceptance_tests`, its oracle tests and the secret-file deny cases. Cost: one small commit. Risk: none for the product; the bootstrap record loses its text pins.
   - (b) Retire only the 28 skipped cases and keep the pins as memory guards. Cost: the same commit. Risk: the pins stay.
   - (c) Keep everything. Cost: noise only.
5. **Recommendation: (a),** confidence high. Classification: no finding of a ticket; cleanup ("Ordinary work: yes; Consequence: other").
6. **What it blocks:** nothing. Waiting costs 28 skips in every record and a little noise.

### P-I1-2. The `docs/source` read deny rule and W1-01's case that pins it (outdated)

1. **Context.** DEC-053 Q5 kept the four originals under `docs/source/`, "excluded from agents (committed `.claude/settings.json` deny rules)". DEC-058 archived `docs/source/`; the folder left the tree on 2026-10-01 (WBS line 1179), and its archive is read with `git show` by exact path (DEC-222, DEC-432). DEC-820 then replaced "every other read restriction beyond the four".
2. **What happened.**
   - The Read and Edit deny rules on `./docs/source/**` survive in `.rulesync/permissions.jsonc` (lines 4, 7) and in the generated settings.
   - My own `ls docs/source` was refused by that rule (refusal 1).
   - The rule makes the sandbox place `docs/source` placeholders in worktrees (row W2-I012).
   - W1-01's `test_docs_source_deny_rules_survive_the_change` (4 cases) asserts the Read and the Edit deny.
3. **Why it matters.**
   - It is a read restriction beyond Policy 2's four, enforced with no recorded reason (DEC-866).
   - It protects a folder that no longer exists.
4. **Options** (DEC-105).
   - (a) Apply now: remove the two rules from `.rulesync/permissions.jsonc`, regenerate the settings (an owner act under CAP-33, which can ride with the settings regeneration after the next re-pin, DEC-884), and retire the 4 cases. Risk: none known.
   - (b) Defer: record now why the deny stands (for example, that `git show` reads of the archive are by exact path only), and keep the case.
   - (c) Re-baseline: keep the Edit rule, drop the Read rule, and revise the case to the Edit half.
5. **Recommendation: (a),** with the regeneration already planned; confidence medium-high. Classification: "Ordinary work: yes; Consequence: other".
6. **What it blocks:** nothing. Waiting keeps a stale refusal and the worktree placeholders.

### P-I1-3. The hook-listing helper's redaction (outdated: 22 functions)

1. **Context.** DEC-525 ordered a helper (`python3 -m gov.guard.hooks`) "so that no session needs to open the settings file", printing hook events with deny lines redacted. DEC-548 and DEC-553 hardened the redaction (bare deny paths, home-folder spellings).
2. **What happened.**
   - Policy 2 opened reads of the settings file and of the held-out list's names: DEC-820 (b), DEC-821 item 3, and W2-58 KPI 5 ("the settings file's reads follow Policy 2").
   - The 22 redaction cases still require that a deny path inside a hook command is printed as `[redacted]`: 1 in `test_w1_02_hook_listing.py`, 9 in `…_paths.py`, 12 in `…_home_paths.py`.
   - Cost: 7.3 s of case time per run.
3. **Why it matters.** The requirement no longer has a basis. The tests pin a behaviour that a simplification could legitimately remove; they fail closed.
4. **Options.**
   - (a) Record that the redaction stands as defence in depth (a register line naming DEC-525 and DEC-548 as still in force for the helper) and keep the cases.
   - (b) Retire the 22 redaction cases. Keep the helper's listing contract (8 cases) while sessions use it.
   - (c) Defer to the next guard ticket.
5. **Recommendation: (a)** if the owner wants the helper to stay discreet, else **(b)**; confidence medium. Either way the register should say which (F-3). Classification: "Ordinary work: yes; Consequence: other".
6. **What it blocks:** nothing.

### P-I1-4. DEC-385 and W1-28's fixture-copy check (outdated: 6 functions, 18 cases)

1. **Context.** DEC-385 (owner, 2026-10-05, stricter-only) followed an incident: 17 copies of `held-out.yaml` were found in test temporary folders (DEC-376). Its rule: "every test fixture that copies the repository leaves `governance/project/held-out.yaml` out".
2. **What happened.**
   - The list's content is the held-out paths, which were then refused to read.
   - Policy 2 made the list's names readable (DEC-820 (b), DEC-821 item 3), and W2-58 opened reads of copies of it (TD-2 (ii)).
   - W1-28's check (`test_w1_28_fixture_copies.py`) still enforces DEC-385 across the whole estate. It reads every file under `tests/acceptance/`, finds whole-tree copiers and runs them on a synthetic repository. So any suite's new copying fixture must take a `root` parameter and leave the file out, or W1-28 goes red.
   - DEC-385's status line has no "Changed by" (F-3).
3. **Why it matters.**
   - A rule with no basis constrains every test designer.
   - It couples W1-28 to every suite's support modules by a file scan W2-66's import graph cannot see (Part 4.1).
4. **Options** (DEC-105).
   - (a) Apply now: mark DEC-385 changed by DEC-820/821, and retire the 18 cases. The fixtures may keep leaving the file out; nothing needs to change in them.
   - (b) Defer: record why DEC-385 stands under Policy 2, and keep the check.
   - (c) Re-baseline: keep only the reading route (whole-tree copies must be runnable) if the owner wants copies of the tree discouraged for cost (lens H finding 2).
5. **Recommendation: (a),** confidence medium-high. Classification: "Ordinary work: yes; Consequence: other".
6. **What it blocks:** nothing. Waiting keeps the coupling for W2-66.

### P-I1-5. Retire the two nested reruns now (duplicates: W1-05's switch-over case, W1-47's builder case)

1. **Context.** DEC-889 item 7 gave "the nested reruns" to W2-67 (test hygiene), after W2-66. DEC-892 a (owner, 2026-10-22) keeps worksteal out of the close, the nightly and the leads "until W2-67 or W2-57 removes the nested runs".
2. **What happened.**
   - `test_the_dependencies_pass_their_acceptance_tests_at_the_switch_over` runs W1-02, W1-03 and W1-04 again in a child pytest. 874.1 s alone in the rehearsal's serial phase: 47 % of that phase, 14.6 minutes of every close, and the slowest case of the estate.
   - Its KPI was a one-time order at W1-05's close (the switch-over after W1-02..04 pass).
   - `test_the_builder_tests_of_the_guard_pass` runs `tests/unit/guard` again (78.2 s in the rehearsal). It timed out at 300 s in the worksteal trial.
   - Both re-check cases the same close already runs in its parallel phase; neither can fail for a fault those cases miss. My six runs of the builder case passed, in 45 to 149 s.
   - W2-67 has no ticket file.
3. **Why it matters.**
   - About 15 minutes of every close's quiet window.
   - Worksteal's measured 9 % stays blocked.
   - Lead verifications run both again serially.
4. **Options.**
   - (a) Retire both now, by the owner, in one test-designer commit (`Rewrite-Reason: retired by the owner, P-I1-5; duplicate of the suites the same run holds`), and remove the switch-over line from `serial-only.txt`.
   - (b) Wait for W2-67. Cost: about 15 minutes per close and worksteal blocked until then.
   - (c) Keep them, but in the nightly only. Cost: they still block worksteal there.
5. **Recommendation: (a),** confidence high. Classification: "Ordinary work: yes; Consequence: other".
6. **What it blocks:** worksteal (DEC-892 a) and about 15 minutes of every close. Waiting costs that per close until W2-67 lands.

### P-I1-6. DEC-769 item 3: what "its own acceptance folder" means for revision work, and W2-21's entry criteria (F-4)

1. **Context.** DEC-769 item 3 (owner): the independent test designer writes only `tests/acceptance/<its WBS id>/`. Folded into W2-21, which is not started.
2. **What happened.**
   - W2-21's card carries the item; its ticket file, sources and allowed paths do not, and the guard rule it needs (`decide.py:153-154`) lies outside them.
   - Five tests pin the old rule (F-4).
   - Revision rounds routinely have a designer edit other tickets' folders (W2-58's designer rewrote W1-02's, W1-50's and W2-50's tests; DEC-874, DEC-875).
3. **Why it matters.** If W2-21 builds item 3 as written, every revision round is refused, and W2-21's own close meets the five pinned tests. If it does not, the decision stays unbuilt with no row.
4. **Options.**
   - (a) A designer's "own folders" are its ticket's own acceptance folder plus the acceptance folders its ticket names in `allowed_paths` (a revision ticket names the folders it revises). The guard reads them from the ticket. W2-21's ticket file gains the KPI, DEC-769 in `sources`, `src/gov/guard/decide.py` (the designer's rule only) in its paths, and the five tests as a revision batch for its test designer (`Rewrite-Reason: DEC-769 item 3`).
   - (b) The sandbox only (W2-21's launcher), the guard unchanged: launched designers are held, an unsandboxed designer subagent is not. Cost: smaller.
   - (c) Re-baseline DEC-769 item 3 to "the folders its ticket names".
5. **Recommendation: (a),** confidence medium. Classification: "Ordinary work: yes; Consequence: fails open (feature-gated: an entry criterion of W2-21, DEC-803)".
6. **What it blocks:** W2-21's test design. Waiting costs nothing until W2-21 starts.

DEC-897 (owner, 2026-10-22) puts W2-69 (ticket `DAEO-rmbe`, opened at 17:14) in the first re-pin; it may change what the secrets check scans (a store's rows as text, the store vacuumed after each rebuild). ### P-I1-8. W1-46's 23 `load_sensitive` entries: the reason text, and the live cases' place (F-1, F-2)

1. **Context.** The 23 entries (DEC-760, DEC-762) say "a live session fails at setup under parallel load and passes alone". DEC-813 keeps them until W2-57; DEC-889 item 2 moves live tests to the nightly; DEC-892 (3) adds a network check before each close. Entries are the owner's commits (DEC-862 (a)).
2. **What happened.**
   - The W1-46 file runs alone in the serial phase.
   - The latest failure (2026-10-21) failed alone too, on the network, and refused W2-63's close.
   - W2-58's five live cases are on neither list and run in the parallel phase.
3. **Why it matters.**
   - A reason that names load points the next reader at the wrong fix.
   - The pre-close check only helps if it tests the route the session uses.
4. **Options.**
   - (a) In W2-57: move every live case (W1-46, W1-50's two, W2-58's five) to the nightly, as DEC-889 item 2 decided. The owner then removes the 23 entries (the list shrinks, DEC-766). Until then, add W2-58's five to the serial list.
   - (b) Keep them in the close, correct the 23 reasons to name the network (an owner commit, validated with the pinned judge's readers before it is sent, DEC-895), and make DEC-892's check probe through a sandboxed session's proxy.
   - (c) As today.
5. **Recommendation: (a),** confidence high; (b)'s reason correction meanwhile, if (a) is more than a week away. Classification: "Ordinary work: yes; Consequence: other".
6. **What it blocks:** nothing. Waiting costs a refused close each time a host is unreachable during a close.

### P-I1-9. Policy 2 (c)'s base branch is written into the guard as `w2/integrate` (F-12): where the fix lands

1. **Context.** DEC-820 (c) and DEC-826 Q2: the test designer is refused the files its ticket's branch changes against its base. The kernel ships this guard to adopted repositories (EXAMIN first, W2-27 to W2-29).
2. **What happened.**
   - The base is a constant, `reads.py:36` `BASE_BRANCH = "w2/integrate"`.
   - Without that branch, `_changed()` returns nothing and (c) refuses nothing for the test designer, silently.
   - W2-58's `test_with_no_base_branch_nothing_is_refused_under_this_item` pins that default.
   - Neither EXAMIN's adoption tickets nor any Wave 3 note name an integration branch.
3. **Why it matters.** In EXAMIN, or in this repository once Wave 3 integrates on another branch, a test designer would read its ticket's implementation with no refusal and no record. That is the independence MR-3 and CAP-38.c rest on.
4. **Options** (a change to the guard, so CAP-33 applies).
   - (a) The base becomes a path-map setting (`integration_branch`). The path map is a shared record that no ticket names (DEC-896 item 2), so the setting itself is a record commit; the guard's reading of it is the ticket's code. The guard reads it; a project that declares Policy 2 (c) without it, or names a branch that does not exist, is refused for the test designer (fail closed, as DEC-218 does for a broken held-out list). W2-58's case is revised with `Rewrite-Reason: DEC-820 (c) for any base`. Carried as an entry criterion of W2-27 (DEC-803), or by the next guard ticket. About 20 lines and a few cases.
   - (b) Document that every adopted repository must name its integration branch `w2/integrate`. Cost: none. Risk: the next wave or repository forgets.
   - (c) Defer to Wave 3's hardening pass. Risk: EXAMIN's adoption in Wave 2 runs with (c) off for test designers.
5. **Recommendation: (a)** as W2-27's entry criterion; confidence medium-high. Classification: "Ordinary work: yes; Consequence: fails open (feature-gated: entry criterion of W2-27, W2-29 and Wave 3's start, DEC-803)".
6. **What it blocks:** EXAMIN's adoption (W2-27). Waiting costs nothing until a repository without `w2/integrate` launches a test designer.
